L1384: 20.2. AGILE
L1385: The open-label extension period of AGILE provided an additional 52 weeks of safety data for participants who completed the SWIFT-1 and SWIFT-2 trials. Data was analyzed in particular for participants who were previously treated with depemokimab in the SWIFT-1 and SWIFT-2 trials and then received additional depemokimab therapy as part of the AGILE trial, referred to as the depemokimab-to-depemokimab treatment group, to determine longer term safety information. General safety, AESIs, and safety review issues identified for SWIFT-1 and SWIFT-2 (e.g., pregnancy, hypersensitivity reactions) were assessed in AGILE.
L1386: General Safety, Laboratory Findings, and Assessment of Drug-Induced Liver Injury
L1387: AEs in the depemokimab-to-depemokimab treatment group (63.2%) were comparable to the placebo-to-depemokimab treatment group (61.4%) and were primarily of mild-moderate severity. SAEs also occurred with similar incidence (7.4% and 7.6% in the depemokimab-to-depemokimab and placebo-to-depemokimab arms, respectively) and there were no on-treatment fatalities. There was no single SAE commonly associated with prolonged depemokimab exposure in the depemokimab-to-depemokimab treatment group. Review of AEs by SOC and OCMQ (narrow) identified a marginal notable treatment difference in the OCMQs systemic hypertension (2.6% depemokimab-to-depemokimab; 1.0% placebo-to-depemokimab) and stroke and transient ischemic attack (1.7% depemokimab-to-depemokimab; 0% placebo-to-depemokimab), though a safety signal was not identified for these AEs in the SWIFT-1/2 trials and the AGILE results are based on interim data. Common AEs occurring with ≥3% frequency in the depemokimab-to-depemokimab and placebo-to-depemokimab treatment groups were nasopharyngitis (11% and 8.1%), upper respiratory tract infection (9.5% and 10%), bronchitis
L1388: (5% and 3.3%), COVID-19 (4.8% and 7.1%), headache (4.1% and 2.9%), respiratory tract infection (3.6% and 0.5%), and pneumonia (3.1% and 2.4%). Of these, AEs that were more common in the depemokimab-to-depemokimab treatment group were nasopharyngitis, bronchitis, headache, respiratory tract infection, and pneumonia. Upper respiratory tract infection was observed in both the SWIFT-1/2 trials and AGILE, though incidence was nearly equal in the depemokimab-to-depemokimab and placebo-to-depemokimab arms, suggesting that prolonged exposure does not increase risk.
L1389: Outlier analysis of general chemistry, liver chemistry, and hematology did not identify any clinically significant findings. Notably, the reduction in total leukocyte counts observed in SWIFT-1/2 due to eosinophil suppression was not as pronounced in AGILE. There were no cases of potential Hy's law. Of mention, the AGILE interim clinical study report errata (dated November 22, 2024) details nine total leukocyte counts in depemokimab participants that were analyzed, but not reported to the investigators or Applicant. Four were outside the normal range (2 below 3.8 G/L and 2 above 10.8 G/L) with one of these considered an outlier (12.7 G/L).
L1390: AESIs and Herpes Infection
L1391: AESIs were as defined in the SWIFT-1/2 trials. There were no AESIs of anaphylaxis or Type III hypersensitivity reaction (immune complex disease/vasculitis). The Applicant categorized one participant in the placebo-to-depemokimab cohort with a Type I allergic hypersensitivity reaction based on the preferred term of pruritus, which occurred 14 days after the first dose of depemokimab. Overall hypersensitivity reactions by OCMQ (narrow) were balanced between depemokimab-to-depemokimab (1.2%) and placebo-to-depemokimab (1.4%) participants. Other systemic reactions were comparable among the depemokimab-to-depemokimab and placebo-to-depemokimab cohorts (&lt;1% incidence each) while injection site reactions were only observed in the depemokimab-to-depemokimab treatment arm at a 1% incidence. Further, the majority of participants in either treatment arm had a decrease, no change, or increase to \leq 450 msec and maximum increase from baseline of \leq 30 msec of their QTcF interval. In the interim clinical study report errata, the Applicant mentions that baseline ECG data from SWIFT-2 participants enrolled in AGILE were missing. Nevertheless, the overall conclusion regarding QTcF assessment remains consistent. There were no depemokimab-to-depemokimab participants with an AE preferred term of QT prolongation based on participant listing. In general, there were no AESIs that occurred with prolonged depemokimab use.
L1392: Regarding herpes zoster and herpes infection, one placebo-to-depemokimab participant with the PT 'herpes zoster' had IMP interrupted. Two participants in the depemokimab-to-depemokimab arm were also diagnosed with 'herpes zoster', one of whom required drug interruption. Four additional depemokimab-to-depemokimab participant had AEs of oral herpes. Consistent with the SWIFT-1 and SWIFT-2 trials, a risk of herpes zoster was not observed.
L1393: Pregnancy
L1394: There was one pregnancy in the depemokimab-to-depemokimab treatment group which was electively terminated with no apparent congenital anomaly present. There was an additional pregnancy in the placebo-to-depemokimab treatment group which was ongoing at the time of the interim data cut-off, but reported as delivered with no congenital anomaly present in the final CSR. See Section 22 for pregnancy associated PMRs to be conducted.
L1395: 20.3. NIMBLE
L1396: The non-inferiority NIMBLE trial provides an additional 52 weeks of supportive safety data for depemokimab and a comparison with other approved anti-IL-5/5R therapies (mepolizumab and benralizumab). General safety, AESIs, and safety review issues identified for SWIFT-1 and SWIFT-2 (e.g., pregnancy, hypersensitivity reactions) were assessed in NIMBLE.
L1397: General Safety, Laboratory Findings, Vital Signs, and Assessment of Drug-Induced Liver Injury
L1398: AEs were comparable between the depemokimab (83.3%), mepolizumab (80.6%), and the benralizumab treatment groups (81.2%), with the majority being mild to moderate severity. SAEs in the depemokimab treatment arm (8.6%) were more common than for mepolizumab (5.9%), but not for benralizumab (10.4%), and comparable to the combined mepolizumab/benralizumab group (8.0%). There were no on-treatment fatalities. There were no SAEs that were observed at a clinically relevant disproportionate rate in depemokimab participants based on SOC, PT, and OCMQ. Common AEs with \geq 5\% frequency and more common in the depemokimab arm compared to the combined mepolizumab and benralizumab arms were pyrexia (5.6% and 3.0%), cough (7.4% and 5.2%), hypertension (5.4% and 3.2%), bronchitis (4.8% and 2.8%), respiratory tract infection (6.3% and 4.5%), dyspnea (5.2% and 4.3%), and sinusitis (6.1% and 5.8%). Of these, pyrexia had a notable imbalance towards depemokimab (5.6%) when compared to the mepolizumab arm (3.1%), benralizumab arm (2.8%), and the combined mepolizumab and benralizumab arm (3.0%). A similar trend was observed for cough, but with a lower difference between the depemokimab (7.4%) and mepolizumab arm (5.6%) than all other comparator arms.
L1399: Outlier analysis of general chemistry, hematology, and vital signs (systolic and diastolic blood pressure, heart rate, and body temperature) did not identify any clinically significant findings. Notably, in a clinical study report errata (dated November 21, 2024) the Applicant notes 21 total leukocyte counts (blinded) that were analyzed, but not reported to the investigators or Applicant. Of these, five were outside the normal range (3 below 3.8 G/L, 2 above 10.8 G/L). There were no potential Hy's law cases in the depemokimab or mepolizumab arm, but one potential case in the benralizumab arm.
L1400: AESIs and Herpes Infection
L1401: There were no AESIs of anaphylaxis or Type III hypersensitivity reactions (immune complex disease/vasculitis). One depemokimab participant had a Type I hypersensitivity reaction (rash), though this occurred with placebo matching benralizumab administration. An additional
L1402: depemokimab participant had two SAEs of anaphylaxis to an unknown trigger and peanut allergen, respectively. Incidence of hypersensitivity by OCMQ (narrow) was balanced between the depemokimab (1.3%) and combined mepolizumab/benralizumab arm (0.9%) with no particular preferred term at a notably disproportionate rate. Other systemic reactions were comparable between the depemokimab cohort (2%) and the combined mepolizumab/benralizumab cohort (&lt;1%) as were local injection site reactions (1% and 3%, respectively, in depemokimab and combined mepolizumab/benralizumab participants). The majority of depemokimab and combined mepolizumab/benralizumab participants had a decrease, no change, or increase to \leq 450 msec and maximum increase from baseline of \leq 30 msec of their QTcF interval. There were no AEs of QTc prolongation by participant listing. Overall, there were no notable AESIs observed with depemokimab compared to other anti-IL-5/5R therapies.
L1403: Incidence of herpes zoster was 0.7% in the depemokimab arm, 1.0% in the mepolizumab arm, 0.8% in the benralizumab arm, and 0.9% in the combined mepolizumab/benralizumab cohort. Moreover, three depemokimab subjects experienced an AE of oral herpes based participant listings. In general, herpes zoster was not more common in depemokimab participants.
L1404: Pregnancy
L1405: There were two pregnancies reported in depemokimab participants which resulted in a spontaneous and elective abortion, respectively. See Section 22 for pregnancy associated PMRs to be conducted.
L1406: 20.4. Overall Safety (Placebo-Controlled Pool; SWIFT-1/2, ANCHOR-1/2)
L1407: The overall safety of depemokimab in all placebo-controlled Phase 3 completed trials across the asthma and CRSwNP indications were evaluated to assess the general safety of depemokimab irrespective of indication and trial population. The four trials (SWIFT-1, SWIFT-2, ANCHOR-1, and ANCHOR-2) were of 52-weeks duration, double-blind, and placebo controlled with depemokimab doses administered subcutaneously at Weeks 0 and 26. Of note, randomization differed between the trials with ratios of 2:1 and 1:1 in the asthma and CRSwNP indications, respectively.
L1408: Based on Applicant provided safety data, on-treatment AEs and SAEs were more common in placebo participants (78% and 10%) compared to depemokimab participants (73% and 5%). Common AEs at \geq 4\% incidence and more common in the depemokimab treatment arm were allergic rhinitis (3.9% v. 1.7%), upper respiratory tract infection (9.6% v. 9.5%), and COVID-19 (14.2% v. 13.2%). There were no adverse events of special interest related to allergic Type 1 hypersensitivity reactions, including anaphylaxis, or Type III hypersensitivity reactions (immune complex disease/vasculitis) seen in either treatment arm. Other systemic reactions was comparable among treatment groups (1% in depemokimab participants, &lt;1% in placebo participants) as were local injection site reactions (1% in depemokimab participants, &lt;1% in
L1409: placebo participants). Evaluation of the abbreviated safety data provided for the placebo-controlled pool did not identify any overarching safety signals.
L1410: 20.5. 120-Day Safety Update
L1411: The Applicant submitted a 120-day safety report on April 9, 2025 which covered trials AGILE and NIMBLE.
L1412: AGILE
L1413: As of December 11, 2024, 8% and 9% of participants experienced SAEs in the depemokimab-to-depemokimab and placebo-to-depemokimab treatment groups, respectively, with no particular SAE by PT occurring at a notably disproportionate rate in either arm. There were 8 new SAEs across 7 participants between August 27, 2024 until December 16, 2024 with no patterns identified and no fatalities. Moreover, there were no AEs leading to treatment discontinuation or study withdrawal, AESIs, or liver stopping events from June 14, 2024 through December 11, 2024. Lastly, there were no new pregnancy cases as of December 16, 2024. In general, the safety profile of longer term depemokimab use remained reassuring.
L1414: NIMBLE
L1415: There were 23 new blinded SAEs across 16 participants between August 27, 2024 to December 16, 2024 with no fatalities. Of these, pneumonia was the most common event reported by SAE and PT. No patterns were identified based on SAEs. No blinded participants not included within the interim analysis met liver stopping criteria as of December 11, 2024. Further, there were no new pregnancy cases as of December 16, 2024 or significant follow-up pregnancy information. Overall, no new safety concerns were identified; however, interpretation was limited based on the blinded treatment allocation.

L1487: 1 Executive Summary
L1488: 1.1 Introduction
L1489: The Sponsor is developing GSK3511294 (depemokimab), a humanized monoclonal antibody that blocks human IL-5 binding to its receptor for the treatment of (b) (4)
L1490: asthma, add-on-maintenance treatment of asthma in adult and pediatric patients aged 12 years and older with type 2 inflammation characterized by an eosinophilic phenotype on medium-to high-dose inhaled corticosteroids (ICS) plus another asthma controller, (b) (4)
L1491: Depemokimab differs from the approved product mepolizumab by only 7 amino acids located in the heavy chain sequence. There were 4 changes made with the goal of increasing affinity for IL-5, and 3 changes were introduced to the Fc region to increase the half-life (YTE modification).
L1492: The focus of this review was to evaluate the extractables and leachables studies with the container closure system as well as a new pharmacology study submitted to the BLA.
L1493: 1.2 Brief Discussion of Nonclinical Findings
L1494: The new pharmacology study indicated that depemokimab did not bind to mouse, rat, rabbit, or dog IL-5.
L1495: The safety evaluation consisted of extractable and leachable studies for depemokimab. The drug product is stored in a clear glass pre-filled syringe (PFS) housed in either an autoinjector (AI) or safety syringe device (SSD) before subcutaneous administration. The Sponsor considered the CCS to be consisted of the syringe stopper, needle shield, and glass syringe with stainless-steel needle, although the needle shield does not come into direct contact with the product. The glass syringe with stainless-steel needle does not contribute organic leachables into the drug product. However, the syringe stopper has the potential to contribute organic leachables.
L1496: Extractable studies were conducted with the syringe stopper and needle shield as well as some manufacturing products that could potentially provide leachables. The Sponsor tested the drug product for leachables following inverted storage for 6 months at 40°C (accelerated degradation) or up to 12 months at 25°C with 24 and 36 time points being planned for September 2025 and 2026.
L1497: The extractables and leachables for the depemokimab drug product did not appear to pose any safety concerns.
L1498: 2 Drug Information
L1499: 2.1 Drug
L1500: Type of Product:BiologicCode/ Generic Name:GSK3511294
L1501: CAS#CAS:2243274-14-6

Structure or Biochemical Description:
Image: Schematic diagram of a humanized monoclonal antibody structure. The diagram shows two heavy (H) chains and two light (L) chains. Each chain consists of variable regions (VH and VL) and constant regions (CH and CL). The variable regions are shown as stacked boxes at the tips of the Fab arms. The constant regions are shown as larger blocks at the base. Disulfide bonds (S-S) are indicated between chains. N-linked glycans are shown as small circles on the Fc constant regions. A legend on the left identifies: Humanized VH-regions, Humanized VL-regions, CDR-regions, Inter Chain Disulphide Bonds (-S-S-), Human CH constant regions, Human CL constant regions, and N-linked glycan (black dot).


Molecular Formula/Molecular Weight:148.7 kDa with carbohydrates

Comment on Impurities:No comments on the impurities.

L1502: 2.2 Relevant IND and BLAs
L1503: IND 146742 Depemokimab GlaxoSmithKline LLC. This was the IND associated with this BLA.
L1504: 2.3 Drug Formulation
L1505: Depemokimab Injection, 100 mg/mL is an aqueous solution containing the following excipients: (b) (4) mM (b) (4) histidine/ L-histidine HCl monohydrate, 40 mM (b) (4) arginine HCl, 180 mM trehalose dihydrate, (b) (4) mM disodium edetate (b) (4) and 0.02% (w/v) polysorbate 80 (PS80) at pH 6.0. A target fill volume of (b) (4) mL is provided in the pre-filled syringe (PFS) to ensure the required 1.0 mL volume is delivered.

L1511: Primary Container Closure System:
L1512: Depemokimab Injection, 100 mg/mL is a product that consists of a single dose sterile liquid drug product (DP) in a clear glass pre-filled syringe (PFS) housed in an autoinjector (AI) or safety syringe device (SSD) that delivers 100 mg of depemokimab for subcutaneous administration. The PFS is a (b) (4) which serves as the primary CCS for the drug product. The PFS is composed of a 1 mL long Type (b) (4) clear glass (b) (4) barrel with a staked 29G (b) (4) x 1 1/2 inch stainless steel needle with a (b) (4) rigid needle shield. The PFS barrel is (b) (4) elastomeric plunger stopper.

L1515: (Excerpted from Sponsor submission)
L1516: Secondary Components:
L1517: The SSD consists of three components (needle guard, plunger rod, and a finger flange) which are assembled with the PFS. The SSD is comprised of a white plunger rod, white finger flange, a grey rigid needle shield and a transparent clear inspection window. The AI consists of (b) (4) that are assembled with the PFS. The AI is comprised of (b) (4) housing with a transparent clear inspection window, a yellow needle guard, and a translucent cap. The drug product contacts only the prefilled syringe components and is not in direct contact with the SSD or AI subassembly components.

L1530: 2.4 Comments on Novel Excipients
L1531: None
L1532: 2.5 Comments on Extractables and Leachables Studies
L1533: The primary container closure is a PFS for both the AI and SSD presentations. A description of packaging components and respective suppliers are shown on the table
L1534: below. The syringe consisted of a clear Type (b)(4) glass barrel with a staked hypodermic needle with a (b)(4) rigid needle shield (b)(4). The plunger stopper is made of (b)(4) elastomer (b)(4).
L1535: (b)(4) These components may contact the drug substance and were identified as potential sources for leachables that could be found in the drug product solution to be injected into the patient. The glass syringe barrel does not contribute organic leachables into the DP; however, the plunger stopper has the potential to contribute leachables.
L1536: Leachables studies were conducted with the drug product in the primary container closure in stability studies following inverted storage for 6 months at 40°C (accelerated degradation) or up to 12 months at 25°C with 24- and 36-month time points being planned for September 2025 and 2026.

L1538: ComponentSubcomponentSupplierMaterial Description1DMF Number

Rigid needle shield (b) (4)

Plunger stopper
L1539: (Excerpted from Sponsor submission)
L1540: Extractables Studies
L1541: Primary CCS
L1542: Evaluation of potential leachables was conducted on 3 of the primary container closure system components (plunger stopper, needle shield, and glass syringe with stainless steel needle) although the drug product is not in direct contact with the needle shield. The qualification threshold was (b)(4) by the PQRI1. Since the greatest patient exposure to the formulation is (b)(4) mL of drug product for (b)(4)
L1543: 1 Safety Thresholds and Best Demonstrated Practices for Extractables and Leachables in Parenteral Drug Products (Intravenous, Subcutaneous, and Intramuscular); PQRI (October 28, 2021) Paskiet et al., The product quality research institute (PQRI) leachables and extractables working group initiatives for parenteral and ophthalmic drug product (PODP). J Pharm Sci and Tech 2013
L1544: mg/day (b) (4) 1 mL syringes), the analytical evaluation threshold (AET) level was calculated to be ~ (b) (4) µg/mL. The extractable compounds that were measured to be \geq (b) (4) % (b) (4) µg/mL) the AET level for volatile compounds or \geq (b) (4) % the AET level (b) (4) µg/mL) for semi-volatile compounds were monitored in the leachables study.
L1545: Methods:
L1546: Syringe Stopper and Needle Shield
L1547: Four different extraction methods were applied to release extractable compounds from the syringe stopper (b) (4) and the needle shield:
L1548: 1. Headspace enrichment over a heated, neat sample preparation.2. Closed vessel extraction at 90°C for 133 hours with 0.01% polysorbate 80 (PS80) in ultrapure water (UPW).3. Closed vessel extraction at 90°C for 133 hours with 0.01% PS80 in UPW/IPA (75/25)4. Closed vessel extraction at 90°C for 133 hours UPW/IPA (75/25).
L1549: The samples were analyzed by:
L1550: 1. Headspace Gas Chromatography / Mass Spectrometry to determine volatile organic compounds (VOC)2. Gas Chromatography/Mass Spectrometry (GC/MS) to determine semi-volatile organic compounds (SVOC)3. High Resolution Accurate Mass (HRAM) Ultra Performance Liquid Chromatography/Mass Spectrometry (UPLC/MS) to determine non-volatile organic compounds (NVOC; syringe stopper only. Non-volatiles were not analyzed for the needle shield).
L1551: Glass Syringe and Stainless-Steel Needle
L1552: The glass syringe and stainless-steel needle were analyzed for elemental impurities in the leachable studies using accelerated degradation (40°C/75% RH). See below under Leachable Studies.
L1553: This component was not subjected to extractables studies like the needle shield and syringe stopper because the glass syringe and stainless-steel needle component were widely used throughout the pharmaceutical industry and were considered by the Sponsor to be inert for leachables, aside from potential elemental impurities. The lack of an extractables study using harsh conditions for the glass syringe was discussed with the CMC team, and they agreed there were no concerns with regarding the extractables and leachables.
L1554: Results:
L1555: The results from the extractable studies were evaluated to identify extractables in the in-process and packaging components for monitoring in leachable studies.

L1629: Leachable Studies
L1630: Testing was conducted to detect leachables migrating from the CCS that a patient could be potentially exposed to assuming standard use of the product. The Sponsor tested the drug product for leachables following inverted storage for up to 12 months at 25°C or 6 months at 40°C (accelerated degradation). The AET was (b) (4) µg/mL for volatile compounds (~ (b) (4) % the qualification threshold) or (b) (4) µg/mL for semi-volatile compounds (~ (b) (4) % the qualification threshold). Non-volatiles were not evaluated as all potential non-volatiles in the extractables study were (b) (4) the reporting threshold (b) (4) µg/day).
L1631: Methods
L1632: The drug product for all 3 batches (GD9B, GD8W, GD8U) was manufactured 1 year prior to T0 (September 22, 2023). They were stored inverted at 5°C, 25°C, and 40°C. Samples were taken at 3, 6, 9, and 12 months with 24 and 36 time points being planned for September 2025 and 2026. The samples stored at 40°C (accelerated degradation) were only stored for up to 6 months. Volatiles analysis was conducted using Headspace GC-MS while semi-volatiles analysis was conducted using direct injection GC-MS.
L1633: An elemental impurities analysis was conducted using samples from depemokimab batches that were inverted for 6 months under accelerated degradation (40°C/75% RH).
L1634: Results
L1635: In the volatiles analysis, (b) (4) was identified at levels exceeding (b) (4) µg/day in all samples under all conditions. The Sponsor noted that (b) (4) is observed (b) (4)
L1636: (b) (4) as opposed to being a leachable from the drug product. The maximum reported level for all other volatile leachables was less than (b) (4) µg/day under all tested conditions.

L1652: A correlation between extractables and leachables could not be established given that there were no volatile or semi-volatile compounds identified as leachables at \geq (b) (4) (for volatile compounds) or (b) (4) % (for semi-volatile compounds) of AET. L1659: 3.3 Previous Reviews Referenced
L1660: Nonclinical studies with depemokimab were evaluated under IND 146742 (submitted to DARRTS January 13, 2021).
L1661: 4 Pharmacology
L1662: 4.1 Primary Pharmacology
L1663: The binding of depemokimab to mouse, rat, rabbit and dog recombinant IL-5 was assessed using SPR on a Biacore™ T200 instrument. Depemokimab (1 µM) did not bind to mouse, rat, rabbit, and dog recombinant IL-5. All positive controls (i.e. reslizumab, AF1964, and TRFK5) bound to their respective IL-5 proteins except for TRFK5 which did not bind to rabbit IL-5. This antibody is an anti-mouse/human IL-5 antibody, and the Sponsor noted that it was not listed as rabbit cross-reactive. Reslizumab was also tested as a positive control for rabbit IL-5 binding but also did not bind.
L1664: Depemokimab bound to human IL-5, but the off-rate was beyond the limits of the Biacore instrument at 25°C and thus kinetics was not reported. Depemokimab did not
L1665: bind to mouse, rat, rabbit, or dog IL-5. Studies reviewed previously (see Nonclinical review for IND 146742 submitted to DARRTS January 13, 2021) reported depemokimab bound to human and monkey IL-5 with similar affinities as evaluated by biacore (K_D of 23.93 and 39.13 pM for monkeys and human, respectively).
L1666: 11 Integrated Summary and Safety Evaluation
L1667: The Sponsor is developing GSK3511294 (depemokimab), a humanized monoclonal antibody that blocks human IL-5 binding to its receptor, for the treatment of (b) (4)
L1668: asthma, add-on-maintenance treatment of asthma in adult and pediatric patients aged 12 years and older with type 2 inflammation characterized by an eosinophilic phenotype on medium-to high-dose inhaled corticosteroids (ICS) plus another asthma controller, (b) (4)
L1669: Depemokimab differs from the approved product mepolizumab by only 7 amino acids located in the heavy chain sequence. There were 4 changes made with the goal of increasing affinity for IL-5, and 3 changes were introduced to the Fc region to increase the half-life (YTE modification).
L1670: The focus of this review was to evaluate the extractables and leachables studies with the container closure system as well as a new pharmacology study with this application.
L1671: The Sponsor provided data for a safety assessment of potential leachables from the primary CCS and manufacturing components for the depemokimab drug product in the prefilled syringe (PFS). This PFS is being planned to be housed in an autoinjector and a safety syringe device. Evaluation of potential leachables through extractable studies was conducted on the plunger stopper and needle shield although the drug product is not in direct contact with the needle shield. The qualification threshold was (b) (4) by the PQRI4.
L1672: In the leachables evaluations, the Sponsor tested the drug product for leachables following inverted storage at 3, 6, 9, and 12 months with 24- and 36-month time points being planned for September 2025 and 2026. The samples stored at 40°C (accelerated degradation) were only stored for up to 6 months. The AET was (b) (4) µg/mL for volatile compounds ((b) (4) % qualification threshold) or (b) (4) µg/mL for semi-volatile compounds (~ (b) (4) % the qualification threshold). Non-volatiles were not evaluated as all potential non-volatiles in the extractables study were (b) (4) the reporting threshold ((b) (4) µg/day). Additionally, samples were analyzed for elemental impurities.
L1673: All detected compounds were (b) (4) the AET in the leachables studies except for (b) (4) which was considered to be (b) (4) as
L1674: 4 Safety Thresholds and Best Demonstrated Practices for Extractables and Leachables in Parenteral Drug Products (Intravenous, Subcutaneous, and Intramuscular); PQRI (October 28, 2021) Paskiet et al., The product quality research institute (PQRI) leachables and extractables working group initiatives for parenteral and ophthalmic drug product (PODP). J Pharm Sci and Tech 2013
L1675: opposed to being a leachable from the drug product. The elemental impurity analysis only detected (b)(4) at levels which would expose patients at ~ (b)(4)-fold lower than the PDE.
L1676: Overall, a correlation for the compounds between extractables and leachables could not be established given the low levels of leachables. The observed quantities of elemental impurities were lower than PDE limits. Further toxicological risk assessment of the extractables and leachables was not considered necessary.
L1677: The new pharmacology study indicated that depemokimab did not bind to mouse, rat, rabbit, or dog IL-5. Studies reviewed previously (see Nonclinical review for IND 146742 submitted to DARRTS January 13, 2021) reported depemokimab bound to human and monkey IL-5 with similar affinities as evaluated by biacore (K_D of 23.93 and 39.13 pM for monkeys and human, respectively). Additionally, previous studies indicated that depemokimab had a slightly higher affinity for human IL-5 compared to mepolizumab (2.31-fold). A 26-week monkey study reviewed previously indicated that the toxicology profile for depemokimab was consistent with IL-5 inhibition.
